=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word) + 1
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

=== PROMPT ===
Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word) + 1
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Let's think deeper: Is the algorithm fundamentally correct? Let's prove.

We maintain j as exclusive right boundary. Invariant before processing i: For every k in (i, n-1]? Actually positions > i, no forbidden word starts at k and is fully contained in [i, j). Equivalently, for all k > i, if there is a forbidden word starting at k of length L, then k+L <= j? Wait fully contained in [i,j) means start >= i and end <= j. Since start k > i, condition k+L <= j. If this held, invalid. We need avoid. The update when processing k set j = k + L - 1 if forbidden at k, so k+L > j? Actually if j = k+L-1, then end index k+L-1, exclusive j = k+L-1, so the forbidden word is not fully contained because its last char index k+L-1 is >= j? Wait slice [i,j) includes indices < j. Last char index = k+L-1. If j = k+L-1, then last char index equals j, so excluded. Thus condition for not containing forbidden word starting at k is j <= k+L-1. So invariant: for all k > i, j <= k + L_min(k) - 1? Actually if multiple forbidden at k, need j <= k + minL -1. The algorithm sets j to min over processed k? Let's see.

At step k, if isForbidden(word[k:j]) returns L (shortest forbidden prefix of current window), then set j = k+L-1. This ensures for that k, j <= k+L-1. For future smaller i, j remains same or decreases, so condition remains. For positions > k processed earlier, j was already <= their threshold; decreasing j preserves. So invariant holds.

At current i, if no forbidden prefix in word[i:j], then no forbidden starts at i. Combined with invariant for >i, window [i,j) contains no forbidden substring. If there is a forbidden prefix length L, then any valid substring starting at i must have exclusive right boundary <= i+L-1. Setting j to that yields the maximum valid right boundary for start i?
```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbidden_set = set(forbidden)
        left = 0
        ans = 0
        for right in range(len(word)):
            for i in range(right, left-1, -1):
                if word[i:right+1] in forbidden_set:
                    left = i+1
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
        return ans
```

But this uses set and slicing up to 10, O(n*10). There is also trie version. The faulty code's algorithm is different but maybe also correct? Let's compare with known solution. Known solution: For each right, maintain left. It checks all substrings ending at right. The faulty code: For each left (from right to left), maintain right. It checks forbidden starting at left. Both are symmetric. It should be correct.

Let's write fixed code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            length = 0
            for idx in range(start, end):
                c = word[idx]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                length += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return length
            return 0

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            L = forbidden_prefix_length(i, j)
            if L:
                j = i + L - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

Need check if `forbidden_prefix_length` returns 0 when no forbidden. In original it returned False, but 0 is falsy. Good.

But wait: If `j` is initialized to n, and `forbidden_prefix_length(i, j)` with `end=j` may be less than `i`? We argued j >= i. Let's prove with code: Initially j=n >= i for all i. If L found, j = i+L-1 >= i. For next iteration i-1, j >= old i > new i, so j > i. So range(start,end) non-empty. Good.

Now, is there any issue with `j = i + L - 1` when L is the length of forbidden prefix but the forbidden prefix may not be fully contained in `word[i:j]` because j is exclusive? We set j to last char index, so slice excludes it. Good.

i=1 L=0, ans=max(1,2)=2.
i=0 L=forbidden_prefix_length(0,3): idx0 c, t=t['c']; idx1 b, t=t['b'], end true, return2. j=0+2-1=1, ans=max(2,1)=2. Return2. Good.

Example word="abcde", forbidden=["bc"]:
n=5,j=5.
i=4 L=0 ans1
i=3 L=0 ans2

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            length = 0
            for idx in range(start, end):
                c = word[idx]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                length += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return length
            return 0

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            L = forbidden_prefix_length(i, j)
            if L:
                j = i + L - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

Now, let's think about if the algorithm's answer for word="abcde", forbidden=["abc","bcd","cde","bc","cd","de","ab","a","e","d","c","b","x","y","z","aa","bb
</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            length = 0
            for idx in range(start, end):
                c = word[idx]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                length += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return length
            return 0

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            L = forbidden_prefix_length(i, j)
            if L:
                j = i + L - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            length = 0
            for idx in range(start, end):
                c = word[idx]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                length += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return length
            return 0

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            L = forbidden_prefix_length(i, j)
            if L:
                j = i + L - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
